Now a round function computes every condition and counts stable rounds in the attempt row (first round, horizon, deadline and the job's stricter values kept at the run's first round, each floored in SQL: horizon at least 10 min, stable at least 3, live at least 1), and the wrapper, which takes only the run id, takes the epoch lock and computes them all again before it calls the owner-only activation and completion. The mode is `AUTOMATIC` because `session_user` is `sagat_handover`; the actor is `system:handover-job` with the run id. `sagat_handover` can make only the automatic handover; the operator's `--apply` runs as the owner. The Rust checks stay as a second line. *Revised after the fourth review (D2271):* `session_user` must be exactly `sagat_handover`, the last stable round younger than a heartbeat, and the run id a `uuid`.
- **D2263** (2026-11-14, third review Major 3, ADR-0091 decision 1) **The run id is the Job's UID.** `helm rollback` to revision N recreates `<release>-schedule-trigger-handover-N`, so a name-based run id would resume an old first round and deadline. The pod reads its label `batch.kubernetes.io/controller-uid` through the downward API into `SAGAT_HANDOVER_RUN_ID`: one UID per Job object, shared by its retried pods, new for a recreated Job. Compose draws a run id at the start of the process (it does not retry the service). Revises D2256.
- **D2264** (2026-11-14, third review Major 2, ADR-0091 decision 1) **Every `migrate` run ensures the handover role and its grants.** `postgres-init` runs only when a volume is first created, so a compose upgrade from 0.2 would never get `sagat_handover`, its `IF EXISTS` grants would be skipped in silence, and a role created later would have none. After the migrations, `migrate` creates the role `NOLOGIN` if it is missing and the migrating role may create roles (otherwise it says the operator must, and goes on), and calls an owner function that grants it everything it needs, every time; given `SAGAT_HANDOVER_PASSWORD` it sets `LOGIN` and the password, quoted by the server. `postgres-init` does not create it, so a fresh volume never needs the password and an operator who opts out never sets one. With the job on and no password, the compose job exits 2 naming the variable. In Helm the operator runs `migrate` with the variable, or creates the login once, as for `sagat_app`. Revises D2252 (OD-2026-11-14-12 said "created like `sagat_app`"; this is a stricter form of it). *Revised after the fourth review (D2269, D2272 to D2275):* the login is set only while `LEGACY` and never after a retirement, from a client-side SCRAM verifier; the password travels apart from the URL; a `42501` warns; a wider role is refused; `NOBYPASSRLS`.
- **D2265** (2026-11-14, third review Minor 4, ADR-0091 decision 1) **The role is retired, not just its Secret deleted.** Deleting a Secret revokes nothing and compose has none. With `--retire-role` (off by default; `schedules.autoHandover.retireRole`, `SAGAT_HANDOVER_RETIRE_ROLE`) the job, after `HANDED_OVER` and the audit, calls an owner function that runs `ALTER ROLE sagat_handover NOLOGIN`; otherwise the runbook gives the statement. With it, a later job that cannot log in (`28000`) exits 0, saying the role is retired. *Revised after the fourth review (D2269, D2270, D2274):* the retirement is recorded in a row `migrate` reads; `28000` may also be `pg_hba`, which the engines' second alert catches.
- **D2266** (2026-11-14, third review Minor 5, ADR-0091 decision 9) **What `sagat_handover` can run is audited, and the command runs as it.** `runtime_privileges.rs` audits only `sagat_app`; a second audit with its own `ALLOWED` list covers the statements of the command for `sagat_handover`, and the auto-handover suites run as that role. `--auto` reads its census through the round function, not the owner-only `schedule_trigger_handover_census` (`handover.rs:364`), and the dry run checks the wrapper's privilege, not `platform_requirement_activate` (`handover.rs:379-386`), which would answer "permission denied" as that role.
- **D2267** (2026-11-14, third review Minor 6, ADR-0091 decision 9) **Every new function pins its `search_path` and is revoked from `PUBLIC`.** `SET search_path = pg_catalog, pg_temp`, `REVOKE ALL ... FROM PUBLIC`, a grant by name, no argument that weakens a condition; a catalog test fails on any `SECURITY DEFINER` function of the schema without the pinned path or executable by `PUBLIC`.
- **D2268** (2026-11-14, third review Minors 7 and 8 and the question on deletion, ADR-0091 decision 1) **The Job's name fits, a drain does not spend its retries, and its pod goes with it.** The name is `<release>-schedule-trigger-handover-<revision>` cut to 52 characters, `-`, and 10 hex digits of the SHA-256 of the whole name, at most 63 (the `job-name` label's limit; a 53-character release name would otherwise exceed it). `restartPolicy: Never`; the `podFailurePolicy` adds `action: Ignore` on the pod condition `DisruptionTarget`, so a drain or a preemption does not count against `backoffLimit: 2`. Helm 3 deletes release resources with background propagation, so the garbage collector deletes the Job's pod after the Job, with SIGTERM and its grace period, and the command starts no activation after SIGTERM (D2251); this is Helm's documented behaviour, not observed here (G2250).
- **D2269** (2026-11-14, fourth review Major, ADR-0091 decision 1) **`migrate` logs the role in only while the state is `LEGACY` and the role was never retired.** In compose `SAGAT_HANDOVER_PASSWORD` stays in `.env` and `migrate` runs at every `up`, so a `migrate` that set `LOGIN` whenever it had the password would undo `--retire-role` at the next start. The retirement is recorded (who, when) in an owner-only singleton that `migrate` reads; `migrate` sets `LOGIN` and the password only when that row is empty and `schedule_trigger_handover.state` is `LEGACY`, and otherwise leaves the role as it is.
- **D2270** (2026-11-14, fourth review, G2251) **The engines alert when the job they expect has written nothing.** `28000` is a retired role and also a `pg_hba` refusal, so with `--retire-role` a bad `pg_hba` rule gives a Job that succeeds and writes no attempt row. Every engine exports `trigger_schedule_handover_attempt_age_seconds` (absent while no row exists) and `trigger_schedule_auto_handover_expected` (from `schedules.autoHandover`, which the chart and compose now also pass to the engines, for this gauge only); `SagatTriggerHandoverNotAttempted` fires while `LEGACY`, expected, and the row is missing or older than 15 minutes, for 30 minutes. Revises "no engine reads either" of D2247 for a gauge that decides nothing.
- **D2271** (2026-11-14, fourth review, D2259) **The wrapper accepts only the role itself, and only a fresh stable round.** It refuses unless `session_user = 'sagat_handover'` exactly (a member of the role logging in as itself has another `session_user`), and unless the run's last stable round is younger than one heartbeat (12 s) on the database's clock, so a stable count from long ago is not spent later.
- **D2272** (2026-11-14, fourth review, D2264) **The password never reaches the server in clear.** `migrate` computes the SCRAM-SHA-256 verifier (`SCRAM-SHA-256$<iterations>:<salt>$<StoredKey>:<ServerKey>`, 4096 iterations, a random salt) and sends `ALTER ROLE sagat_handover LOGIN PASSWORD '<verifier>'`, which PostgreSQL stores as given; a clear password in the statement could be logged by `log_statement` or a PL/pgSQL error context. The verifier is built with the hashing the workspace already has, or a reviewed dependency (T018-084). What is left is G2254.
- **D2273** (2026-11-14, fourth review, D2264) **The job's password is not part of an interpolated URL.** `DATABASE_URL` for the job and for `migrate`'s role step carries no password; `SAGAT_HANDOVER_PASSWORD` is passed apart and set on the connection options, so `@`, `/` or `#` in it cannot break the URL. Helm puts the password in its own key of the Secret.
- **D2274** (2026-11-14, fourth review, D2264, D2265) **Where PostgreSQL 16 refuses to alter the role, `migrate` and the job warn and go on.** From PostgreSQL 16 a `CREATEROLE` role may alter only roles it holds `ADMIN OPTION` on, so a `sagat_handover` the operator created makes the password step and the retirement answer `42501`. `migrate` warns with the remedy (`GRANT sagat_handover TO <migrating role> WITH ADMIN OPTION`, or the statement run by the role's creator) and goes on; the job, after a handover, warns the same and still exits 0. Neither fails the deployment for it.
- **D2275** (2026-11-14, fourth review nits, D2264) **The role is exactly as narrow as decided, or the grant function refuses.** `NOSUPERUSER NOCREATEDB NOCREATEROLE NOREPLICATION NOBYPASSRLS`, a member of no role; the grant function that `migrate` calls every run raises (and `migrate` reports it) when an existing `sagat_handover` has another attribute or a membership, instead of granting to a role wider than the design.
- **D2260** (2026-11-14, T018-060, T018-061, SC-018-005, OD-2026-11-13-8) **The released-upgrade scenario `cron-trigger-upgrade` uses the released legacy cron pass itself, not current code standing for it.** The released side is the `EventDispatcher` of the v0.2.0 `World` (`tests/fixtures/release-upgrade/cron_trigger.rs`): the production crates of `bfda3420`, wired as the v0.2.0 engine wires it (`with_cron(CronCalendar)`) on the runtime role, compiled by `scripts/test-release-upgrade-profile.py` from the immutable tree. It is not v0.2.0's `serve`: the coordinator has to know which pass reaches a tick first, so the released process ticks only when asked over the private control socket. The triggers are daily (midnight UTC), and a tick is made due by moving `next_fire_at` (and, where the schedule pass is meant to reach it, `next_action_at` and `next_actual_at`) into the past by SQL, as the fault suite does; no test waits for a real tick. The run of a tick is found by its `cron.fired` event id (`cron:{trigger}:{epoch}`) and its input must equal, as JSON, what the current `cron_document` makes for that nominal time and its `firedAt`; in the run of 2026-11-14 it was equal to the byte as well, for the released run and the three current ones.
- **D2261** (2026-11-14, T018-060, T018-061, OD-2026-11-13-8) **The current side of the scenario is the real engine with the default configuration, restarted between steps.** `sagat_execution_engine::serve` runs in the test process with the support's test settings, the runtime role and no `SAGAT_SCHEDULES_ENABLED` (the test asserts the variable is absent, that `EngineConfig` reads `schedules_enabled` as true, and that the engine registers `schedules-v1`). It is stopped (a clean stop gives its shards back) and started again between the steps, so that a step that needs "the released pass first" or "the current schedule pass first" has it; one step (4c) leaves both processes up and asks the released pass to tick while the engine polls, with no barrier. After the handover the stop of the legacy pass is shown by a current `EventDispatcher` built as `serve` builds it, freshly (no cached state): its round starts nothing and leaves the trigger's `next_fire_at` where it was, then the engine's schedule pass starts the tick (`STARTED`, provenance the schedule) and the trigger is still not moved. The handover is `run_handover_command` (what `control-plane schedule-trigger-handover` prints) as the owner, `--dry-run` and then `--apply`, `--expect-live 1`, after `schedule_trigger_drift` is empty. **Red first:** with `ScheduleAdmission::tick` made to return `None` (the schedule pass no longer shares feature 004's identity), the scenario failed at step 4b, the released pass started a second run of the tick the current schedule pass had started (`left: 1, right: 0`, "the released pass finds the run under the shared key"); reverted, it passed.
- **D2262** (2026-11-14, T018-061, runbook `schedules.md` 6.2 step 1) **The released process stays up through the migration and is stopped and joined before the handover.** That is the rolling-upgrade shape the runbook describes (migrate while old engines run; attest none runs before the handover). Found on 2026-11-14: the v0.2.0 legacy pass works on the migrated schema (`20270116000000`) with connections opened before the migration: it fires a due tick, and on a tick the current schedule pass already started it starts nothing and moves the trigger on without a backoff, so it reads the key the schedule pass wrote as its own.
- **D2280** (2026-11-14, T018-083, ADR-0091 decisions 1 and 6) **`schedules.autoHandover` is a map, and the boolean the ADR names is its opt-out.** The ADR names both `schedules.autoHandover: false` and `schedules.autoHandover.databaseSecret` / `.retireRole`, which one YAML key cannot be at once. The chart reads the key as a map (`enabled`, default true; `databaseSecret`; `retireRole`; `stable`; `horizonSeconds`; `waitSeconds`; `minLive`, 0 for the engine's replicas or the autoscaler's minimum) and also takes the boolean `false` (or `true`) in its place; each key given replaces its default one by one (a merge would read `false` as a value not given). `contract_scalability_ha::handover_chart` renders both forms of the opt-out. Helm logs `cannot overwrite table with non table` for the boolean form (the default is a map) and still uses it; the runbook names `enabled: false` first.
- **D2281** (2026-11-14, T018-084, D2272) **The password of `sagat_handover` is printable ASCII without spaces, 16 to 1024 characters.** PostgreSQL normalises a password with SASLprep before hashing it at login; the verifier `migrate` computes does not, so only a password SASLprep leaves unchanged is accepted, and a short one is refused because a verifier in a log allows an offline guess (G2254). The runbook gives a generator. HMAC-SHA-256 and PBKDF2 are written over the workspace's `sha2` and held to RFC 4231 and the published PBKDF2-SHA-256 vectors; the verifier is proved by logging in with it.
- **D2282** *(superseded by D2289, OD-2026-11-15-1: the deferred check stays as ADR-0082 wrote it)* (2026-11-14, T018-082, ADR-0091 decision 9, found by `trigger_auto_handover`) **The deferred check that a requirement moved the epoch runs as its owner.** `platform_requirements_epoch_moved()` is a deferred constraint trigger: it runs at the commit, outside the wrapper, with the privileges of the session, so the job's commit failed `42501` on `platform_requirement_epoch`. The migration makes it `SECURITY DEFINER` with the pinned path (it reads one row and can only refuse a commit) instead of granting `sagat_handover` the epoch; `down` makes it invoker again.
- **D2283** (2026-11-14, T018-085, D2267) **The catalog test holds every definer to a pinned path, and every function of ADR-0091 to exactly `pg_catalog, pg_temp`.** Three definers older than ADR-0091 pin `pg_catalog, public, pg_temp` (G2280); the test accepts that form for a definer only while PUBLIC cannot create in `public`, and fails any definer executable by PUBLIC.
- **D2284** (2026-11-14, T018-082, ADR-0091 decisions 2 and 5) **What a round reports while it counts, and what the wrapper says of a fleet not yet still.** A round in which every condition holds reports no reason while it counts (every series of `trigger_schedule_handover_blocked` at 0), `FIRST_HORIZON` within the first horizon, and `FLEET_CHANGING` when the live set changed; the wrapper refuses too few stable rounds and a last stable round older than 12 s as `FLEET_CHANGING`, the closed set having no reason of its own for them. A round sooner than 15 s after the last one counted counts nothing and ends nothing.
- **D2285** *(superseded by D2290, OD-2026-11-15-1: the round function keeps its five arguments)* (2026-11-14, T018-082, T018-084, ADR-0091 decisions 4, 5 and 9) **The job writes what only it sees through the round function.** `BUSY`, `CHECK_TOO_SLOW`, `INTERRUPTED` and `DEADLINE` are seen by the job, not the database; the round function takes them as a sixth argument (`p_note`, the closed set of those four), writes them as the round's reason and counts nothing, so the four functions of decision 9 suffice. A note does not end the streak: what a wait cures is retried with it.
- **D2286** (2026-11-14, T018-082, OD-2026-11-14-7) **The operator's handover records the same evidence.** `schedule_trigger_handover_complete` (replaced, same signature) and the wrapper share one internal move, `schedule_trigger_handover_record(actor, mode, horizon)`: the census copy, the tenant set and the mode (`OPERATOR` for the command, `AUTOMATIC` for the wrapper, whose session must be `sagat_handover`), keyed by the epoch. `--apply` completes the tenant audit after its commit and on "already handed over", as the job does; the census copy of the operator covers the 10-minute floor.
- **D2287** (2026-11-14, T018-082, ADR-0091 decision 5) **One attempt row per run id; the engines read the most recent.** The rows of runs a week old are deleted by the round, sixteen at a time; `sagat_app` and `sagat_handover` read the table, nobody writes it but the round function.
- **D2288** (2026-11-14, T018-083, ADR-0091 decision 5) **The engines read the handover's view at most every 15 s**, at the start of each round of the trigger pass (a replica holding no shard included), and export the gauges from it; a failure to read leaves them as they were and is logged. An engine with `SAGAT_SCHEDULES_ENABLED=false` runs no trigger pass and exports none of them.
- **D2289** (2026-11-15, OD-2026-11-15-1, T018-082, ADR-0091 decision 9, supersedes D2282) **`sagat_handover` holds `SELECT` on the one column ADR-0082's deferred check reads.** `platform_requirements_epoch_moved()` runs at the commit of the job's activation with the session's privileges and reads `platform_requirement_epoch.changed_xact`; the grant function gives the role `SELECT (changed_xact)` on that table and nothing else of it, and revokes it from a wider role. The check is left an invoker function. Held by `runtime_privileges::every_statement_the_handover_job_issues_is_within_sagat_handovers_privileges` (the check's needs read from its body, the column and not the table) and `trigger_auto_handover::sagat_handover_can_run_its_five_functions_read_its_two_tables_and_one_column_and_nothing_else`.
- **D2290** (2026-11-15, OD-2026-11-15-1, T018-082, T018-084, supersedes D2285) **The stop note is a function of its own.** `schedule_trigger_handover_note(run, reason, stable, horizon, min_live, wait)`: `SECURITY DEFINER`, pinned path, revoked from PUBLIC, executable by `sagat_handover` only; the reason is the closed set `BUSY`, `CHECK_TOO_SLOW`, `INTERRUPTED`, `DEADLINE` (`22023` otherwise); it opens the run's row as a round does (a run whose first round timed out still has one), writes the last reason and counts nothing. The round function keeps its five arguments. The two share an internal, owner-only `schedule_trigger_handover_attempt_open`.
- **D2291** (2026-11-15, security review m3) **The owner's floor of the live engines.** `schedule_trigger_handover_floor` (owner-only singleton) is written by `migrate` from `SAGAT_HANDOVER_MIN_LIVE` through `schedule_trigger_handover_set_floor`; the round and the wrapper count with the greater of the run's `--min-live` and the floor, so a caller of the round function cannot choose fewer. The chart cannot write it (it holds no owner credential, G2287).
- **D2292** (2026-11-15, security review m4) **The runs are bounded and the engines read every live one.** A new run id is refused (`54000`, `TOO_MANY_RUNS`, exit 2) while sixteen others are before their deadline and not stopped (`INTERRUPTED`, `DEADLINE`); the engines export the reasons of every run before its deadline and of the most recent run, and the age of the most recent round.
- **D2293** (2026-11-15, security review M1 and m6) **Narrowness is one function, asked before any grant and any login.** `schedule_trigger_handover_role_narrow()` adds, to D2275, a member of `sagat_handover` that may `SET` it or `INHERIT` its privileges (on PostgreSQL 16; any member before 16); a member with `ADMIN OPTION` alone, which PostgreSQL 16 records for the role's creator, can only grant it and is accepted. `set_handover_login` asks it in its transaction and answers `LoginSet::Wider` without altering anything. The remedy for `42501` is therefore `GRANT sagat_handover TO <migrating role> WITH ADMIN OPTION, INHERIT FALSE, SET FALSE`.
- **D2294** (2026-11-15, correctness review) **A committed handover exits 0 whatever its audit does.** `--apply` and `--auto` warn on `AUDIT_CONFLICT` or a failure of the audit after the commit, exit 0, and do not retire the role (the retirement needs the audit); the engines read the count of tenants left from the view `schedule_trigger_handover_audit_pending` (owner's rights, a count and never an audit id, `SELECT` to `sagat_app`) as `trigger_schedule_handover_audit_pending`, and `SagatTriggerHandoverAuditPending` fires after thirty minutes. A record differing only by its trace id is a conflict.
- **D2295** (2026-11-15, correctness review Majors 1 and 2, architecture review minor) **The job keeps its own deadline and its own counts.** From its start plus `--wait`, then from each round's report of the run's deadline; every wait is clamped to it, and the job ends `DEADLINE` (exit 3) even when no round answers or the database cannot be reached (a lost connection is retried with the backoff meanwhile). Statement timeouts of the rounds and of the activation are counted apart, each reset only by its own kind of call, three in a row `CHECK_TOO_SLOW`. `--wait` must exceed the horizon plus 15 s per stable round. The activation's bounds are part of the request (a test narrows them to reach the statement timeout).
- **D2296** (2026-11-15, security review m5, correctness review, architecture review Major 1) **Retirement leaves no password, and the job is switched off after it.** The retirement runs `NOLOGIN PASSWORD NULL` and ends the role's other sessions (best effort). A role with no password is refused `28P01`, which is never a retirement (only `28000` is), so a later job exits 2; the runbook and the release notes say to set `schedules.autoHandover.enabled: false` (or `SAGAT_SCHEDULES_AUTO_HANDOVER=false`) after the handover, before the Secret is deleted, and the chart's references to the Secret are `optional`, so a Job rendered without it exits 2 at once instead of a pod that never starts. The chart cannot read the state of the handover, so it does not leave the Job out by itself (G2288).
- **D2297** (2026-11-15, correctness review) **A streak with a gap is not consecutive.** A round more than two heartbeats (30 s) after the last one counted starts the streak over.
- **D2298** (2026-11-15, architecture review Majors 2 and 3, security review m7) **The audits read what each side runs.** The owner's side (`ensure_handover_role`, the floor, the login) is `crates/infra-postgres/src/handover_role.rs`; the privilege audit of `sagat_handover` reads `handover_auto.rs` and excuses nothing (`HANDOVER_ALLOWED` is empty). The catalog test names the three functions of G2280 and fails when one is fixed, and also fails on a role other than the owner that may create in `public` and on any sequence privilege of `sagat_handover`. An unknown outcome of the role functions is an error.
- **D2299** (2026-11-15, security review m2 and nit) **The verifier is logged nowhere by the client, and the secrets are zeroed.** The statement that sets the password runs on a connection of its own with statement logging off (sqlx would log it at `DEBUG`, or at `WARN` when slow); the clear password, the salted password and the client key are held in `Zeroizing` and wiped when dropped. Held by `trigger_handover_role` (a `TRACE` capture of the client's log during `migrate` holds no verifier).
- **D2304** (2026-11-15, CI of pull request 226, D2264) **The role step of `migrate` holds an advisory lock of its own.** Every `migrate` ensures `sagat_handover` and grants it again, and four runners started at once (`e2e_platform_foundation` `us03::migrating_twice_changes_nothing_and_the_api_comes_up_healthy`) failed one time in five with `tuple concurrently updated`: two `GRANT`s on the same objects at once. `ensure_handover_role`, `set_handover_floor` and `set_handover_login` now each run in a transaction that first takes `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended('sagat.migrate.handover-role', 0))`, so a second runner waits. *Test:* `trigger_handover_role::concurrent_runners_take_turns_on_the_role_step_and_both_succeed` holds that lock and sees the second runner wait on it in `pg_locks`, then both succeed (red first: the second runner never waited, the stage timed out). `us03` then passed 10 of 10 locally where it had failed 1 of 5.
- **D2300** (2026-11-14, T018-072, NFR-018-005, G2050, OD-2026-11-13-8) **The idle cost of the schedule passes is measured on the engine's own wiring and switch.** The scenarios `idle` and `idle-throughput` (`tests/performance/schedules-bulk-operations/idle.rs`) build the two loops of `PASS_SCHEDULES` as `serve` builds them (a `SchedulePass` over `ScheduleFiring`, a `TriggerPass` over `TriggerReconciler` with `TriggerSettings::default()`, one `DatabaseClock`, over the pool and the run store the run and worker services use) and drive them with `loops::run_schedules`, `loops::run_triggers` and their round functions. The configuration is `EngineConfig::from_lookup` over the process's environment (so `SAGAT_SCHEDULES_POLL_MS` and `SAGAT_SCHEDULER_DISCOVERY_REST_MS` act as in `serve`); "on" is the variable absent, "off" is `false`, and `settings::optional_passes` decides whether the loops start. The cost of a window is its rate less the mean of a quiet window before and one after; the throughput compares segments in pairs whose order alternates, so a drift of the machine's load falls on both sides.
- **D2301** (2026-11-14, T018-072) **`run.sh` preloads `pg_stat_statements` only when the selection names `idle`** (`SAGAT_PERF_PG_STATEMENTS=1` or `0` overrides it). The statement counts need it; the other scenarios keep the server the report declares, so their figures stay comparable with the run of 2026-11-09. The idle figures were taken with it loaded in both modes; its own overhead is not measured (G2300).
- **D2302** (2026-11-14, OD-2026-11-13-8, NFR-018-005) **The idle cost does not stand against the default of 0.3.0, and no engine code is changed.** Measured (the report, section of 2026-11-14): with no schedule and no cron trigger, the two loops add 5.2 statements, 3.9 transactions, 0.18 ms of database execution, 0.6 to 0.8 ms of the engine's CPU and about 1 ms of the database container's CPU a second, all index reads that find nothing, the same at 2 and at 100 tenants; every bound held in 3 of 3. Beside a load of runs the difference is below what the machine resolves (median pair ratio on / off 1.04, G2301). The cost is per replica and is avoided by `SAGAT_SCHEDULES_ENABLED=false`. Nothing in the data is a cost problem that calls for a fix; what grows is not idle (G2302).
- **D2303** (2026-11-14, T018-072) **The throughput comparison is reported as "no measurable effect", not as a figure of the cost.** Its pairs varied from 0.90 to 1.39 at a load average of 18 to 22, as wide as two segments of the same mode, so it cannot resolve a few percent; its bound (median pair at least 0.95) held in 3 of 3 with two repetitions within 1.5% of it. The cost is stated from the idle windows, which resolve a fraction of a statement a second, and the share of the load is an estimate from transactions (at most about 2%), not a measurement.
- **D2305** (2026-11-15, T018-071, D2085) **The alert of the schedule pass's lag is the shipped `SagatSchedulerLate`; the task's `SagatSchedulesLate` is read as it.** `SagatSchedulerLate` (`max(scheduler_lag_seconds) > 60` for five minutes) is in `infra/observability/alerts.yaml` of the tags `v0.1.0` and `v0.2.0` (`git show v0.2.0:infra/observability/alerts.yaml`, line 166), so operators' silences, routes and dashboards name it; an alert that shipped in a release is not renamed. Its summary names the schedule, batch and reset passes (T018-071's note of 2026-11-05); the name the task's text gives (`SagatSchedulesLate`, a plan draft) is read as the shipped one. No new rule is added. *Confirmed by the owner 2026-11-15 (OD-2026-11-15-15, "Manter o nome lançado").*
- **D2306** (2026-11-15, T018-087, security review of 2026-11-15, finding 3) **The `down` of `20270117000000_trigger_handover_automatic` takes back the role's grants in its database and leaves the role's login to the operator.** The review asked the `down` to run `ALTER ROLE sagat_handover NOLOGIN PASSWORD NULL`, since it drops the retirement row that keeps `migrate` from giving a login back. Built that way, it broke the suites: `sagat_handover` is a role of the cluster, every rollback test of `migration.rs` runs this `down` on the shared test cluster, and the auto-handover tests logging in at the same moment failed with `28P01` (`trigger_auto_handover::a_crash_after_the_commit_…`, seen once). A schema `down` that alters a role of the cluster acts on every database the role serves, which is the same reason the `down` never dropped it. So the `down` is unchanged in that respect, its header says why, and `docs/runbooks/schedules.md` 6.4 gives the operator's statement (`ALTER ROLE sagat_handover NOLOGIN PASSWORD NULL`, as the role's creator, and the Secret deleted). *Test:* `trigger_handover_role::a_role_that_can_create_in_a_schema_or_owns_the_database_is_wider_and_loses_its_login` runs the `down` on a PostgreSQL of its own, reads the statement from the runbook, runs it and sees the password refused. What a role left with a login can do after the `down` is what PUBLIC can (connect, temporary tables), plus a schema it may create in, which finding 1 now refuses at every `migrate` before.
- **D2307** (2026-11-15, G2248, T018-085, correctness review of 2026-11-15, M1 and M2) **The tenant set, the move under the lock and the tenant audit, measured at 100,000 cron triggers of 1,000 tenants.** The scenario `triggers-tenant-set` (`tests/performance/schedules-bulk-operations/triggers.rs`, `tenant_set`; `run.sh` lists it) seeds 100,000 converged triggers over 1,000 tenants and, in 20 transactions rolled back, times the statement that records the tenant set and the whole move (`schedule_trigger_handover_record`: the census, the unconverged check, the tenant set, the state); then the unconverged check alone (the wrapper makes it once more before the move, M1), and, after a committed handover, the completion of every tenant's audit record (one statement, M2). Three repetitions, as `sagat_app` where the scenario connects as the runtime role, debug build of the client, the suites' PostgreSQL 16 container, a loaded machine (load average 7 to 8.5): **tenant set p95 15.4, 15.4 and 15.3 ms** (bound 100 ms, held 3 of 3: the owner is not asked); **the move p50 272 to 329 ms, slowest 355 to 369 ms** (bound 3 s); **the unconverged check p50 267 to 300 ms, slowest 340 to 349 ms**, so the wrapper's two checks are about 0.7 s of its 3 s; **the audit of 1,000 tenants 43.4 to 43.6 ms** (bound 5 s). The figures are in `tests/performance/reports/schedules-bulk-operations.md` (section of 2026-11-15). Not run through `run.sh` (a release build of hours on a shared machine); a tenant set above 1,000 tenants is not measured (G2309).
- **D2308** (2026-11-15, T018-084, G2307) **Every refusal call site of the handover commands names the test that holds its exit code, and a fitness test holds the list.** 32 call sites of `declined(`, `denied()`, `deadline(`, `deadline_unreached(` and `interrupted()` in `apps/control-plane/src/handover.rs` and `handover_auto.rs` (not 16: the task counted the operator's path before `--auto` was built) each carry `// refusal test: NAME`; `handover_auto::tests::every_refusal_call_site_names_a_test_that_exists` finds every such line outside the tests, requires the marker and a `fn NAME(` under `tests/` or `src/`, and asserts the count, so a new site without a test fails (hand mutant: a marker removed, red). 27 sites name a test that reaches them and asserts the code (the RunError variant or `exit_code()`); new tests for the sites none reached: `handover::tests::an_apply_without_an_operator_is_refused_before_the_database_is_reached`, `trigger_handover::schedules_v1_required_of_other_kinds_refuses_the_handover_in_words`, and in `trigger_auto_handover`: `a_database_the_job_cannot_reach_ends_it_at_its_deadline_with_three_or_at_an_interrupt_with_four`, `a_role_other_than_sagat_handover_is_refused_at_the_round_or_at_the_activation_with_exit_two`, `a_state_this_build_does_not_know_stops_the_job_at_its_activation_or_its_round_with_exit_two`, `a_run_whose_deadline_the_database_says_has_passed_ends_with_exit_three`, `an_interrupt_during_the_stable_round_starts_no_activation_and_exits_four`, `schedules_v1_required_of_other_kinds_ends_the_job_with_exit_two`; strengthened with the code or the message of their own branch: `trigger_round3::the_function_moves_only_the_state_legacy`, `trigger_round3::a_long_publication_…`, `trigger_auto_handover::a_seventeenth_live_run_…`. **Five sites, all of the operator's `--apply`, are marked `none` with the reason** (a connection that fails to be taken right after a rollback; a schedule made between the rollback under the lock and the read after it; the defensive `--operator` check a dry run never reaches; the step's own state conflict, told after the judge's reason; `HANDOVER_UNCONVERGED`, which the census under the same lock refuses first): no seam reaches them without a fault injection the command does not have.
- **D2309** (2026-11-15, T018-084, G2307) **`--run-id UUID` is the flag of the run id, `SAGAT_HANDOVER_RUN_ID` its environment form.** `cli::parse` takes `--run-id` with `--auto` only (a usage error otherwise, or for a value that is not a uuid); `handover_auto::run_id` takes the flag, or the variable, or draws one, and refuses (exit 2) a flag and a variable that differ, since which run a retried pod resumes must not depend on which of the two a deployment sets. The chart keeps the variable from the controller UID (D2263). *Tests:* `cli::tests::the_run_id_is_a_flag_of_the_job_and_a_uuid`, `handover_auto::tests::the_run_id_is_the_flag_or_the_variable_and_never_two_that_differ` (hand mutants: the flag not parsed, the difference not refused: both red), and the built binary is given `--run-id` in `trigger_auto_handover::a_real_sigterm_or_sigint_…`.
- **D2310** (2026-11-15, T018-083, T018-085, G2247, G2283, G2286, G2305) **The job's process is tested as a process, and compose waits on health.** (1) `raise_on_signals` installs the SIGTERM and SIGINT handlers before it returns (it used to install them in the task that waits, on its first poll), so once the job has written a round a signal is the interrupt. `trigger_auto_handover::a_real_sigterm_or_sigint_to_the_built_binary_between_its_rounds_exits_four_with_interrupted_and_nothing_recorded` starts the built `control-plane` with `--auto --run-id`, waits for its first round (`FIRST_HORIZON` in the attempt row: the barrier), sends a real `kill -TERM`, then on a new database `kill -INT`: exit 4, `INTERRUPTED`, `LEGACY`, nothing recorded (hand mutant: no handler, red with the signal's default action). (2) `the_gauges_of_the_handover_are_read_from_a_running_engines_page_before_during_and_after_the_job` starts the built `execution-engine` (its admin address read from its own log line) and scrapes `/metrics`: the twelve reasons and two modes at zero, the age absent, `trigger_schedule_auto_handover_expected 1`, then `FIRST_HORIZON` at 1 with an age after a round as `sagat_handover`, then `AUTOMATIC` at 1 and every reason at zero after the wrapper hands over, and 0 from an engine told the job is off (hand mutant: a reason never set, red). Both need the binaries built (`cargo build -p sagat-control-plane --bin control-plane -p sagat-execution-engine --bin execution-engine`), which the CI integration job now does. (3) The compose service waits for `service_healthy` of the control plane and the engine (both images have a `HEALTHCHECK` of their `healthcheck` subcommand) and switches off the image's probe for itself; `contract_scalability_ha::handover_chart::the_compose_service_…` holds both (red first on `service_started`). (4) `scripts/smoke-compose.sh` runs the job: without `SAGAT_HANDOVER_PASSWORD` it exits 2 naming it; with one (drawn for the stack, `migrate` run again) it writes its first round, the first round is moved back by a horizon as the owner, and it hands over (exit 0, `HANDED_OVER AUTOMATIC`); a second run says already handed over, and switched off it exits 0.
- **D2311** (2026-11-15, T018-087, G2306) **The reviews of 2026-11-15 on the diff of the build, and what answered each finding.** The `security-reviewer` and the `correctness-reviewer` read the diff against `develop` with ADR-0091 as the spec; their reports and the answer to each finding are in `reviews/2026-11-15/`. Fixed, each after a test seen red: a role that may create in a schema or owns a database is wider (`schedule_trigger_handover_role_narrow`), and a role found wider loses its login (security 1, 2); the member test asserts the wrapper's own message, the privilege audit names the real test, the job's password is zeroized (security nits); three step bounds of the activation are `CHECK_TOO_SLOW` (correctness m1); once handed over a round is answered before the cap of sixteen runs (m2); `trigger_schedule_auto_handover_expected` is exported by every engine (m3, part).
