| G2403 | **A database clock that steps back between a run's commit and a job's creation leaves that run out of the job's selection, even when the job names it by id.** A job selects the runs with `created_at <= selection_bound_at` (D1033), and both instants are the database's `clock_timestamp()`: the run's read in its creating transaction just before the row is inserted (`execution.rs`, `create`), the job's when it is created, before its transaction has an identity (`batch.rs`, `create`). The horizon waits for the transactions that were open at creation, not for the ones that had committed: a run committed before the job whose `created_at` is later than the bound, because the clock was set back in between, is never selected, and a run-id selector (`Selector::run_ids`) applies the same bound, so a job asked to act on that very run selects nothing and ends `COMPLETED`. D1033 names only the other side: the process clocks of transactions that commit later. *Likelihood:* it needs the clock of the database host to step backwards, which a production server does at most on a large correction (chrony and NTP slew small offsets); the Colima VM of a laptop does it every ten seconds (its guest agent set the clock back to the host's by 110 to 150 ms, and by up to 2 seconds for two hours, on 2026-11-26), where it failed the fault matrix of the last item once (`last item cut off: selected`, 0 for 1; the fault tests now date their runs an hour back, `runs_started` in `tests/fault/schedules-bulk-operations/batch.rs`). | (a) The bound is the later of the clock and the latest `created_at` the creating snapshot sees in the namespace: every committed run is inside, but it needs an index on `runs (tenant_id, namespace_id, created_at)`, a migration and its write cost on every start. (b) Order by transaction instead of time: an `xid8` (or commit-order) column on `runs`, or the creating snapshot (`pg_current_snapshot()`) captured with the bound, so that a run is selected when its transaction is visible in the job's snapshot and not by its timestamp: exact, and it removes the clock from the rule, at the cost of a column or a snapshot kept on the job and a selection statement that reads it. (c) A job that names its runs by id selects them by id with no time bound (the runs it names exist, or the selection finds none of them): the case a person sees is closed at no cost, and a filter selector keeps the bound and the gap. (d) Accept it and say so in D1033 and the contract. *Recommended:* (c) now, which closes the case where the person named the run, and (b) with the snapshot for the filter selectors when the selection is next changed, which makes the rule independent of any clock; (a) costs an index on the hottest table for a rarer case. | **Option (c) built** (D2405, 2026-11-26, the coordinating session's decision for release 0.3.0): a job whose selector names its runs by id selects them with no time bound, and a backward step of the clock no longer leaves a named run out. **Option (b) stays open** for the filter selectors, which keep the bound and this gap. |

## 5. Owner questions (the plan implements the recommended defaults)

**Answered 2026-10-29** by the owner, who approved this feature and accepted the recommended default of
each question (`docs/owner-decisions.md`): **O1000 (a)** pause with an audited system note; **O1001** 30 days,
namespace-configurable up to 366; **O1002 (a)** `run:batch` for admin and operator only. The three
paragraphs below are kept as the question that was put.

- **O1000 — Schedule authority after its authorizing subject loses access.** (a) Pause with an audited
  system note at the next action; (b) keep firing under a namespace service identity.
  **Recommended: (a)** — fails closed (constitution V); (b) needs a service-principal model that does
  not exist.
- **O1001 — How long a closed business id is remembered.** 7, 30 or 366 days, or per namespace up to a
  ceiling. **Recommended: 30 days default, namespace-configurable up to 366** (Temporal's example
  retention; finite ceilings).
- **O1002 — Who holds `run:batch`.** (a) admin and operator; (b) also developer for actions a developer
  may take one by one. **Recommended: (a)** — a batch multiplies an action's blast radius by up to
  100,000.
- **O1951 — Must the effect on a run and the record of its batch item be one transaction?** (a) Keep the two keyed
  transactions with the look before the effect (D1960, G1961): a stale replica or a cancel can let one more keyed effect
  through, and an item can say less than happened to its run; or (b) pass a hook into the four single-run use cases so
  the fence, the job and the item are locked and the outcome recorded inside the run transaction, as ADR-0050 decision 2
  says. **Recommended: (b) before the feature is released; (a) is what is built** and is safe against a repeat, not
  against one extra effect after a cancel. **Answered 2026-11-13 (OD-2026-11-13-2): (b), to be built before the feature is switched on.** Built the same day (D2219, G1961 closed).
- **O1950 — Who may write a `CANCEL_OTHER` schedule.** (a) whoever holds `run:operate` (admin, operator; of the roles
  that write schedules only admin), because the schedule cancels runs for them; (b) whoever holds `schedule:write`
  (admin, developer), the schedule cancelling as the platform. **Recommended and implemented: (a)** — a developer who
  may not cancel a run by hand would otherwise cancel any run of a workflow by writing a schedule (D1953). **Answered 2026-11-13 (OD-2026-11-13-2): (a), as built.**
- **O1953 — How many failed selection rounds does a batch job survive before it ends `SELECTION_FAILED`?** (a) ten, with the wait of
  a refused item (5 s doubling to 5 min): the job ends about 25 minutes after its first failed round, at the cost of at most eleven
  statements of ten seconds; (b) fewer (five: about 3 minutes; a transient database trouble of a few minutes then ends jobs people
  made); (c) more or a window in hours (a job whose selector the database cannot read stays active, and holds its place against the
  active-job ceilings, for longer). **Recommended and implemented: (a)**, the retries of an item (D2197): the plan implements the
  recommended default; accept or change it. The owner decides a product default; it is not recorded in `docs/owner-decisions.md`. **Answered 2026-11-13 (OD-2026-11-13-2): (a), ten rounds.**
- **O1952 — Which failure states must the browser journeys of T018-074 show?** The journeys show every refusal the real platform gives through the Studio's own controls (a name in use, a quota of zero, a stale revision, no run matched, a count capped, items skipped and failed with their reasons, the acknowledgement missing in the dialog, the identifier held by an open run, a finished run that remembers it, the limit of waiting resets, a reset that could not be made). Four failure states of the task's brief are not shown (G2034, G2035): the preview refused for a selector that is too wide (the Studio builds none), `RESET_POINT_NOT_ELIGIBLE` and `RESET_REEXECUTION_NOT_ACKNOWLEDGED` from the platform (the Studio does not send a request that gets them), `SOURCE_DID_NOT_END` (an hour at the least) and `AUTHORITY_REVOKED` (a member of the journey's own). (a) Accept their words in `ResetRun.test.tsx` and the REST suites as the evidence, and close T018-074 on the journeys it has; (b) add the journeys that can be made, with `page.route` altering the request the Studio sends (a refusal of the real platform for a request the Studio would not make), a member the harness mints, and an engine whose expiry is at its one-hour minimum and a clock that can move. **Recommended: (a) for the first three, (b) for `AUTHORITY_REVOKED` if the harness can mint a member.** T018-074 is *narrowed pending the owner* until it is answered. **Answered 2026-11-13 (OD-2026-11-13-2): (a); T018-074 closes on the journeys it has.**
- **O1954 — May an operator keep the handover of the cron triggers manual?** (a) Yes, with a setting, on by default; (b) no.
  **Recommended: (a).** **Answered 2026-11-14 (OD-2026-11-14-6): (a), `schedules.autoHandover: false` (Helm) or its compose twin
  (D2247).**
- **O1955 — Do tenants see the handover in their audit?** (a) One audit row per tenant that has a cron trigger; (b) only the
  platform record. **Recommended: (a).** **Answered 2026-11-14 (OD-2026-11-14-7): (a), written after the commit and keyed by its
  epoch (D2246).**
- **O1956 — May the Helm hook that waits for the handover fail the release?** The owner chose a `post-upgrade` hook
  (OD-2026-11-14-5). A hook makes `helm upgrade` wait for it and a failed hook fails the release (and, with `--atomic`, rolls it
  back). (a) Keep the hook; the job waits at most `--wait`, the runbook says to give `helm upgrade` a `--timeout` longer than that,
  and a job that reaches its deadline fails the release with the reason on its last line; (b) keep the hook with a short wait
  (minutes) and exit 0 when it has not handed over, leaving the alert to say why: the release never fails for it, and the handover
  then waits for the next deployment or the operator; (c) a plain Job named by the release's revision, not a hook: Helm does not
  wait for it and the release never fails for it. **Recommended: (a)**: a release that says it failed while its handover did not
  happen is the honest answer, and the rollback `--atomic` makes is safe before a handover. G2246. **Withdrawn 2026-11-14 after the second review: the Job is no longer a hook (D2251, O1957).**
- **O1957 — May the automatic handover depart from the letter of OD-2026-11-14-5 in two ways?** The second review (Majors 1 and 2)
  found that a `post-upgrade` hook makes `helm upgrade` fail and outlives a rollback, and that the owner's credential would sit in
  the cluster at every upgrade. (a) A plain Job of the release named by its revision (D2251) and the narrow role `sagat_handover`
  (D2252), which can record `schedules-v1` and nothing else; (b) the hook and the owner's credential as decided, with a `helm
  upgrade --timeout` longer than the wait and the owner Secret deleted after. **Recommended: (a).** ADR-0091 and T018-082 to
  T018-087 are written for (a).
  **Answered 2026-11-14 (OD-2026-11-14-12): "Aceitar as duas"**: the plain Job per revision and the `sagat_handover` role, as D2251 and D2252 describe.
- **O1958 — Is a batch job kept for at least 14 days after it ended, even past its window and past the 90-day ceiling?** The window of a job is
  its `expiresAt` (30 days from its creation by default, never fewer than 14 and never more than 90, D2324, D2326). The purge removes a job
  when it has finished, `expiresAt` has passed **and it ended at least 14 days ago** (D2325), so a job that ends after its own window (one
  that waited for an engine that was off, or for a transaction that did not end) outlives it: a 30-day job that ends on day 16 lives to day
  30, one that ends on day 28 to day 42, and a 90-day job that ends on day 80 to day 94, which is past the ceiling of
  FR-018-033 ("at most 90 days") for that job. A job that ends in time (every job that ends within its window less 14 days) is removed at
  its `expiresAt`, as a person was told. (a) **Keep the floor** (what is built): a person who looks for what a late job did still finds it,
  and the key of the request that made it (7 days) is forgotten before it is purged; the ceiling of 90 days is a ceiling on the window a job
  is stamped with, not on how long it lives; (b) **drop the floor**: a job is purged at `expiresAt` whenever it ended, the ceiling holds
  for every job, and a job that ends late may be purged minutes after it ended (then the 14-day floor of the stamp, D2326, keeps only the
  key guarantee, which needs the other half: a job not eligible before `finished_at` plus 7 days); (c) **count the window from the end**
  (`finished_at` plus the window): the ceiling is 90 days after the end for every job, a job lives its whole window after it ended, and
  `expiresAt` is then the earliest instant it may go and not the instant it goes. **Recommended and built: (a).** FR-018-033 carries one
  sentence for it, marked pending this question. Not recorded in `docs/owner-decisions.md`.
